The basilica can seat 3,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religious_congregations">Religious congregations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_of_Lisieux&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Religious congregations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>The Oblates de Ste. Thérèse<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> were founded in 1933 by Gabriel Martin, a priest in the diocese of Luçon (France) and Béatrix Douillard.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> Their mission is to evangelize in the parishes and to help Saint Thérèse to "spend her heaven by doing good on earth".<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Inline_citation#When_you_must_use_inline_citations" title="Wikipedia:Inline citation"><span title="The text near this tag needs a citation. (October 2022)">This quote needs a citation</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/w/index.php?title=Congregation_of_Saint_Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_of_Lisieux_-_CST&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Congregation of Saint Thérèse of Lisieux - CST (page does not exist)">Congregation of Saint Thérèse of Lisieux - CST</a> was founded on March 19, 1931, by <a href="/w/index.php?title=Fr._Basilius_Panatt_CST&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Fr. Basilius Panatt CST (page does not exist)">Fr. Basilius Panatt CST</a>, with permission from <a href="/wiki/Mar_Augustine_Kandathil" class="mw-redirect" title="Mar Augustine Kandathil">Mar Augustine Kandathil</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_bishop" title="Metropolitan bishop">Metropolitan</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Syro-Malabar_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Syro-Malabar Catholic Church">Catholic St. Thomas Christians</a>, as the first Indian religious order for brothers.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Theresienschr%C3%A4in.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Theresienschr%C3%A4in.jpg/220px-Theresienschr%C3%A4in.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Theresienschr%C3%A4in.jpg/330px-Theresienschr%C3%A4in.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Theresienschr%C3%A4in.jpg/440px-Theresienschr%C3%A4in.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="1600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Theresienschr%C3%A4in.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Reliquiary in the Carmel in Lisieux</div></div></div>
<h3><span id="Works_inspired_by_Th.C3.A9r.C3.A8se"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Works_inspired_by_Thérèse">Works inspired by Thérèse</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_of_Lisieux&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Works inspired by Thérèse">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><b>In films</b>
</p>
<ul><li>1929: <a href="/wiki/Julien_Duvivier" title="Julien Duvivier">Julien Duvivier</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/La_Vie_miraculeuse_de_Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_Martin" title="La Vie miraculeuse de Thérèse Martin">La Vie miraculeuse de Thérèse Martin</a></i> ("The Miraculous Life of Thérèse Martin"), with <a href="/wiki/Simone_Bourday" title="Simone Bourday">Simone Bourday</a> as Thérèse.<sup id="cite_ref-IMDb_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IMDb-146">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>1952: <a href="/wiki/Andr%C3%A9_Haguet" title="André Haguet">André Haguet</a>, <i>Procès au Vatican</i> ("Trial at the Vatican"),<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> life of Thérèse based on original documents in consultation with the abbé Combes.</li>
<li>1964: <a href="/wiki/Philippe_Agostini" title="Philippe Agostini">Philippe Agostini</a>, <i>Le Vrai Visage de Thérèse of Lisieux</i> ("The True Face of Thérèse of Lisieux"), <a href="/wiki/Short_film" title="Short film">short</a> <a href="/wiki/Documentary_film" title="Documentary film">documentary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>1986: <a href="/wiki/Alain_Cavalier" title="Alain Cavalier">Alain Cavalier</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_(film)" title="Thérèse (film)">Thérèse</a></i>, biographical evocation with <a href="/wiki/Catherine_Mouchet" title="Catherine Mouchet">Catherine Mouchet</a> as Thérèse, a film rewarded in 1987 with 6 <a href="/wiki/C%C3%A9sar_Award" class="mw-redirect" title="César Award">César Awards</a> including the <a href="/wiki/C%C3%A9sar_Award_for_Best_Film" title="César Award for Best Film">César Award for Best Film</a>.</li>
<li>2004: Leonardo Defilippis, <i>Thérèse: The Story of Saint Thérèse of Lisieux</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-IMDb_146-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IMDb-146">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p><b>In music</b>
</p>
<ul><li>An opera, <i>Thérèse</i>, based on her life, was composed by English composer <a href="/wiki/John_Tavener" title="John Tavener">John Tavener</a> in 1969, shortly before his conversion to Eastern Orthodoxy.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>In 1973, Brazilian composer José Antônio de <a href="/wiki/Almeida_Prado" class="mw-redirect" title="Almeida Prado">Almeida Prado</a> composed the oratorio <i>Thérèse, l'Amour de Dieu</i>, for speakers, soloists, chorus and orchestra, based on texts by Thérèse organized by <a href="/w/index.php?title=Henri_Doublier&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Henri Doublier (page does not exist)">Henri Doublier</a>. The work was commissioned by the French Ministry of Culture and premiered in Rio de Janeiro in 1975.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The Carmelite monk and musician <a href="/wiki/Pierre_%C3%89liane" title="Pierre Éliane">Pierre Éliane</a> has released four discs on the poetry of Therese. <i>Thérèse songs</i>, three discs from 1992 to 1994, and <i>Sainte Therese de Lisieux – poesies</i> (1997). The original texts are sung in full over melodies composed by Pierre Éliane.</li>
<li>In 2013 <a href="/wiki/Gr%C3%A9goire_(musician)" title="Grégoire (musician)">Grégoire</a> set some of the poems of Thérèse to music in an album called <i><a href="/wiki/Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_%E2%80%93_Vivre_d%27amour" title="Thérèse – Vivre d&#39;amour">Thérèse – Vivre d'amour</a></i>, with collaborating artists <a href="/wiki/Natasha_St-Pier" title="Natasha St-Pier">Natasha St-Pier</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anggun" title="Anggun">Anggun</a>, <a href="/wiki/Michael_Lonsdale" title="Michael Lonsdale">Michael Lonsdale</a>, <a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gr%C3%A9gory_Turpin" class="extiw" title="fr:Grégory Turpin">Grégory Turpin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Les_Stentors" title="Les Stentors">Les Stentors</a>, <a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonia_Lacen" class="extiw" title="fr:Sonia Lacen">Sonia Lacen</a>, <a href="/wiki/Elisa_Tovati" title="Elisa Tovati">Elisa Tovati</a>, <a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Michel_di_Falco" class="extiw" title="fr:Jean-Michel di Falco">Monseigneur di Falco</a> and <a href="/wiki/The_Little_Singers_of_Paris" title="The Little Singers of Paris">The Little Singers of Paris</a>.</li>
<li>The Chairman Dances’ song “Thérèse”<sup id="cite_ref-Bandcamp_151-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bandcamp-151">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> (2016) “describes the beginning of the end for Saint Thérèse of Lisieux, who woke to find blood on her handkerchief, a sure sign of... tuberculosis.”<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Missa Sanctae Theresiae ab Infante Iesu</i> by <a href="/wiki/Serban_Nichifor" class="mw-redirect" title="Serban Nichifor">Serban Nichifor</a>, June 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Devotees_of_Therese">Devotees of Therese</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_of_Lisieux&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Devotees of Therese">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:182px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kostel_Nejsv%C4%9Bt%C4%9Bj%C5%A1%C3%AD_Trojice_(Fulnek)_%E2%80%93_idt-003.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Kostel_Nejsv%C4%9Bt%C4%9Bj%C5%A1%C3%AD_Trojice_%28Fulnek%29_%E2%80%93_idt-003.jpg/180px-Kostel_Nejsv%C4%9Bt%C4%9Bj%C5%A1%C3%AD_Trojice_%28Fulnek%29_%E2%80%93_idt-003.jpg" decoding="async" width="180" height="240" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Kostel_Nejsv%C4%9Bt%C4%9Bj%C5%A1%C3%AD_Trojice_%28Fulnek%29_%E2%80%93_idt-003.jpg/270px-Kostel_Nejsv%C4%9Bt%C4%9Bj%C5%A1%C3%AD_Trojice_%28Fulnek%29_%E2%80%93_idt-003.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Kostel_Nejsv%C4%9Bt%C4%9Bj%C5%A1%C3%AD_Trojice_%28Fulnek%29_%E2%80%93_idt-003.jpg/360px-Kostel_Nejsv%C4%9Bt%C4%9Bj%C5%A1%C3%AD_Trojice_%28Fulnek%29_%E2%80%93_idt-003.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1704" data-file-height="2272" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kostel_Nejsv%C4%9Bt%C4%9Bj%C5%A1%C3%AD_Trojice_(Fulnek)_%E2%80%93_idt-003.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Statue of Saint Thérèse at the <a href="/wiki/Most_Holy_Trinity_Church,_Fulnek" class="mw-redirect" title="Most Holy Trinity Church, Fulnek">Most Holy Trinity Church, Fulnek</a>, <a href="/wiki/Czech_Republic" title="Czech Republic">Czech Republic</a></div></div></div>
<p>Over the years, a number of prominent people have become devotees of Saint Therese of Lisieux.
</p><p>These include (but are not limited to) the following names listed in alphabetical order (either "name in religion" or "first name + surname").
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Religious_personalities">Religious personalities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_of_Lisieux&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Religious personalities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p><b>Women</b>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Alphonsa_of_the_Immaculate_Conception" title="Alphonsa of the Immaculate Conception">Alphonsa of the Immaculate Conception</a>  or Saint Alphonsa - Nun of the <a href="/wiki/Poor_Clares" title="Poor Clares">Poor Clares</a>, one of the <a href="/wiki/Enclosed_religious_orders" title="Enclosed religious orders">enclosed religious orders</a>.  She is the first Indian woman to be canonized in 2008 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Dina_B%C3%A9langer" title="Dina Bélanger">Dina Bélanger</a> - Canadian (<a href="/wiki/Provinces_and_territories_of_Canada" title="Provinces and territories of Canada">province</a> of <a href="/wiki/Quebec" title="Quebec">Quebec</a>), <a href="/wiki/Nun" title="Nun">professed religious</a> and a member of the <a href="/wiki/Religious_of_Jesus_and_Mary" title="Religious of Jesus and Mary">Religieuses de Jésus-Marie</a>. Feeling very close to Thérèse of Lisieux, whom she considers a model, she followed her in the "<i>little way</i>", even though she was not a <a href="/wiki/Carmelites" title="Carmelites">carmelite</a>. She said: «Therese had to lead me in the way of love and abandonment, to take care of the inner workings of my soul (...) Through her intercession, she opened to me the garden of trust. Then I tasted the true fruit of abandonment. Moreover, she made me advance in the spirit of childhood (...). And all her actions, needless to say, bear the stamp of love».<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> She was beatified in 1993 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Maria_Candida_of_the_Eucharist" title="Maria Candida of the Eucharist">Maria Candida of the Eucharist</a> - Was inspired by reading <i><a href="/wiki/The_Story_of_a_Soul" class="mw-redirect" title="The Story of a Soul">The Story of a Soul</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Mother_Teresa" title="Mother Teresa">Mother Teresa</a> of Calcutta, originally called Agnes, explained her choice of the name Teresa as follows: "I chose Therese as my namesake because she did ordinary things with extraordinary love"<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> She and Therese were both deeply drawn by the words of Christ on the Cross: "I thirst.".<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p><b>Men</b>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Francis_Bourne" title="Francis Bourne">Francis Bourne</a> - British Cardinal - "I love St Therese of Lisieux very much because she has simplified things: in our relationship with God she has done away with the mathematics…".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGaucher1987&#91;&#91;Category:Wikipedia_articles_needing_page_number_citations_from_April_2021&#93;&#93;&lt;sup_class=&quot;noprint_Inline-Template_&quot;_style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;&#91;&#91;Wikipedia:Citing_sources&#124;&lt;span_title=&quot;This_citation_requires_a_reference_to_the_specific_page_or_range_of_pages_in_which_the_material_appears.&amp;#32;(April_2021)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;&#93;&#93;&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGaucher1987[[Category:Wikipedia_articles_needing_page_number_citations_from_April_2021]]&lt;sup_class=&quot;noprint_Inline-Template_&quot;_style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;[[Wikipedia:Citing_sources|&lt;span_title=&quot;This_citation_requires_a_reference_to_the_specific_page_or_range_of_pages_in_which_the_material_appears.&amp;#32;(April_2021)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;]]&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;-159">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Daniel_Brottier" title="Daniel Brottier">Daniel Brottier</a> - "In 1923, Father Brottier's superiors from the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Ghost_Fathers" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Ghost Fathers">Congregation of the Holy Spirit</a> gave him the responsibility to resume [the] great Work of the Orphan-Apprentices of Auteuil. The former military chaplain already had great devotion to the little Carmelite. At the time of his appointment in <a href="/wiki/Auteuil-Neuilly-Passy" class="mw-redirect" title="Auteuil-Neuilly-Passy">Auteuil</a> <a href="/wiki/Paris" title="Paris">Paris</a>, he decided to build a chapel in honor of Therese who had just been beatified a few months earlier, so that the orphans could pray to their <i>little mama</i> in a sanctuary worthy of her."<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> - "When I have a problem I ask the saint, not to solve it, but to take it in her hands and help me accept it."<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_I" title="Pope John Paul I">Pope John Paul I</a> - "Dear little Thérèse, I was seventeen when I read your autobiography. It struck me forcibly ... Once you had chosen the path of complete dedication to God, nothing could stop you: not illness, nor opposition from outside, nor the mists or inner darkness."<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Inline_citation#When_you_must_use_inline_citations" title="Wikipedia:Inline citation"><span title="The text near this tag needs a citation. (October 2022)">This quote needs a citation</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Maximilian_Kolbe" title="Maximilian Kolbe">Maximilian Kolbe</a> offered his first Mass for the intention of the beatification and canonization of Sister Therese of the Child Jesus and the Holy Face. He also dedicated his Asian missions to Therese.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Marie-Joseph_Lagrange" title="Marie-Joseph Lagrange">Marie-Joseph Lagrange</a> - founder of Biblical School in Jerusalem - "I owe to Saint Therese the fact that I didn't become a bookworm. I owe her everything because without her, I would have shrivelled up, my mind dried up."<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Inline_citation#When_you_must_use_inline_citations" title="Wikipedia:Inline citation"><span title="The text near this tag needs a citation. (October 2022)">This quote needs a citation</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Alfredo_Obviar" title="Alfredo Obviar">Alfredo Obviar</a> - Filipino Bishop and founder of the Missionary Catechists of St. Therese of the Infant Jesus.  <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> named him as <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a> in 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Don_Luigi_Orione" class="mw-redirect" title="Don Luigi Orione">Don Luigi Orione</a> - Italian priest, founder of the <a href="/wiki/Sons_of_Divine_Providence" title="Sons of Divine Providence">Sons of Divine Providence</a>, a clerical congregation of Pontifical Right for men founded in 1903.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Pio_of_Pietrelcina" class="mw-redirect" title="Pio of Pietrelcina">Pio of Pietrelcina</a>, known as «&#160;Padre Pio&#160;» - Italian <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Friars_Minor_Capuchin" title="Order of Friars Minor Capuchin">Franciscan Capuchin</a>,  stigmatist, and mystic.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Marcel_Van" class="mw-redirect" title="Marcel Van">Marcel Van</a>, <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a>, a Vietnamese <a href="/wiki/Redemptorist" class="mw-redirect" title="Redemptorist">Redemptorist</a> brother. He allegedly had visions of and conversations with Thérèse. He was heavily influenced by her spirituality, and his teachings are often considered a continuation of her "Little Way."</li></ul>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Lay_personalities">Lay personalities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_of_Lisieux&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Lay personalities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p><b>Women</b>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Louise_de_Bettignies" title="Louise de Bettignies">Louise de Bettignies</a> - French secret agent who spied on the Germans for the British during World War I. She received a Catholic education&#160;: she did her secondary studies with the  <a href="/wiki/Sisters_of_the_Sacred_Heart" title="Sisters of the Sacred Heart">Sisters of the Sacred Heart</a> in <a href="/wiki/Valenciennes" title="Valenciennes">Valenciennes</a>, France,  and her higher studies with the <a href="/wiki/Ursulines" title="Ursulines">Ursulines</a>  at <a href="/wiki/Upton,_Bexley" title="Upton, Bexley">Upton</a> then with the Ursulines at <a href="/wiki/Wimbledon,_London" title="Wimbledon, London">Wimbledon</a><sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Benedetta_Bianchi_Porro" title="Benedetta Bianchi Porro">Benedetta Bianchi Porro</a> - Italian laywoman and mystic, whose sarcophagus in Dovadola includes a quote of Therese, writing «I am not dying, but I am entering life.», and who was read the Act of Oblation to Merciful Love on her own Dies natalis on January 23, 1964. She was proclaimed <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a> in 1993 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Louise_Brooks" title="Louise Brooks">Louise Brooks</a> - American dancer and actress - "Her spiritual trek was guided by two New York City priests, whom she saw with increasing frequency in late 1952 and early 1953, and by a book about the life of Saint Therese of Lisieux, <i>Storm of Glory</i> by John Beevers. So enamored of Saint Therese was Louise that she spent one entire Sunday propped up in bed with her easel, fashioning a portrait in charcoal on canvas from a small photo of Therese at eight. It was the best and most haunting of her dozen works of art.".<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Lucie_Delarue-Mardrus" title="Lucie Delarue-Mardrus">Lucie Delarue-Mardrus</a> - French writer - "the Carmelite-apparition … appeared, roses in hand, in the midst of an era which grieves and terrifies poets … Thérèse is my fellow-countrywoman, and almost my contemporary. I do not wish to let her glorious entry into sanctity pass by without honoring her in my own way. And besides, she is henceforth public property." (Introducing her book, 1926).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The citation is not specific enough to facilitate source verification (October 2022)">not specific enough to verify</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Gwen_John" title="Gwen John">Gwen John</a> - Welsh painter "Some of her final paintings were in fact of religious subjects [including] countless (over 700) tiny ink copies after a photograph of Thérèse of Lisieux and the saint's elder sister".<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Claudia_Koll" title="Claudia Koll">Claudia Koll</a> - Italian, initially an actress, she later became a devout Roman Catholic involved in several humanitarian activities.(<span style="color:blue"><i>Concerning her devotion  to Thérèse of Lisieux</i> </span>&#160;:<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>).</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ada_Negri" title="Ada Negri">Ada Negri</a> - Italian poet and writer.(<span style="color:blue"><i>Concerning her devotion  to Thérèse of Lisieux</i> </span>&#160;:<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>).</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Edith_Piaf" class="mw-redirect" title="Edith Piaf">Edith Piaf</a> - French singer - "Shortly after her birth Edith developed a cataract. She was blind for almost three years. Her grandmother, Louise, took her to Lisieux. She saw. It was a real miracle for Edith. She always believed this. Since that time she had a real devotion to St Therese of the Child Jesus … she always had a small picture of the saint on her bedside table." (Simone Berteaut, Edith Piaf's closest friend).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGaucher1987224_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGaucher1987224-168">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Marthe_Robin" title="Marthe Robin">Marthe Robin</a> - French Roman Catholic mystic and stigmatist. She said that «&#160;on October 3, 1926,  the first liturgical Feast of Thérèse of Lisieux, she appeared to her three times, telling her that she would not die, that she would live and that she would continue her mission through foundations throughout the world.&#160;».<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> She was declared <a href="/wiki/The_Venerable" title="The Venerable">venerable</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> on November 7, 2014.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Vita_Sackville-West" title="Vita Sackville-West">Vita Sackville-West</a>, author of <i>The Eagle and the Dove</i> a study of Thérèse of Lisieux and Teresa of Avila - admired the "tough core of heroism" she found in the pages of <i>Histoire d'une âme</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Anna_Sch%C3%A4ffer" title="Anna Schäffer">Anna Schäffer</a> - German woman invalid and mystical. Born into a poor family, she began working at a young age, hoping in her heart to become a nun. At the age of 16, she received a message from Heaven for the first time, a Saint appeared to her (she did not know his name) and told her that she would experience great suffering.Three years later, in 1901, she was severely burned and had to stay in bed permanently. Thus she was unable to fulfil her dream of becoming a nun.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>  She was canonized by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> in 2012.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Delia_Smith" title="Delia Smith">Delia Smith</a> - British cookery writer - "Therese … not only personified the first <a href="/wiki/Beatitude" class="mw-redirect" title="Beatitude">beatitude</a> but is, I am deeply convinced, the supreme teacher in regard to the spiritual life."<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p><b>Men</b> 
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Henri_Bergson" title="Henri Bergson">Henri Bergson</a> - Nobel prize winner - "One reason why the philosopher Henri Bergson esteemed Therese so highly was that he was fascinated by the qualities of character which prompted her to confront the Pope of her day, <a href="/wiki/Leo_XIII" class="mw-redirect" title="Leo XIII">Leo XIII</a>, in pursuit of her own desires … explicitly forbidden by the chaplain to address Leo XIII, Therese flouted the injunction..she was dragged away by two papal guards. This is hardly the simpering and docile saint which Therese's statuary too often suggests."<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Georges_Bernanos" title="Georges Bernanos">Georges Bernanos</a> - "A few months before her death, Therese wrote of 'a wall rising up as far as the heavens … when I sing of the happiness of Heaven, I feel no joy, because I am simply singing of what I WANT TO BELIEVE' (Manuscripts, 248) … Bernanos, a devotee of Thérèse, employs the same image in his novel <i><a href="/wiki/Diary_of_a_Country_Priest" title="Diary of a Country Priest">Diary of a Country Priest</a></i>, where the priest confides to his diary, <i>Behind me there was nothing, and in front of me a wall, a black wall</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Gilbert_Cesbron" title="Gilbert Cesbron">Gilbert Cesbron</a> - French novelist  of Catholic inspiration. In 1952 he wrote a play called <i>Breaking the statue</i>, telling the story of Thérèse.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jacques_Fesch" title="Jacques Fesch">Jacques Fesch</a> - Murderer of a French police officer. He was sentenced to death and executed in 1957. While in prison, he became a devout Roman Catholic. In 1987 the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Paris" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Paris">Archbishop of Paris</a>, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Jean-Marie_Lustiger" title="Jean-Marie Lustiger">Jean-Marie Lustiger</a> opened the cause for his beatification in 1993. But this proposal has been controversial, as some Catholics have argued that, given his crimes, he cannot serve as a model, while others believe that he is an example of conversion.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Henri_Gh%C3%A9on" title="Henri Ghéon">Henri Ghéon</a> - French playwright, poet, writer and critic, wrote a book entitled <i>Sainte Thérèse</i> (1934).<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jean_Guitton" title="Jean Guitton">Jean Guitton</a> - French Catholic philosopher and theologian. He expressed his attachment to Thérèse of Lisieux in several of his works, notably in <i>Portrait de <a href="/wiki/Marthe_Robin" title="Marthe Robin">Marthe Robin</a></i><sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Brian_Desmond_Hurst" title="Brian Desmond Hurst">Brian Desmond Hurst</a> – American film director. (<span style="color:blue"><i>Concerning his devotion  to Thérèse of Lisieux</i> </span>:<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>).</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jack_Kerouac" title="Jack Kerouac">Jack Kerouac</a> - American novelist, had a boyhood habit of praying to Saint Thérèse and was an altar boy.<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Charles_Maurras" title="Charles Maurras">Charles Maurras</a> - French author, journalist, poet and political philosopher: a prolific writer who was very controversial because of his political commitment and writings, which had a real influence on the intellectual world of his time. Initially <a href="/wiki/Agnosticism" title="Agnosticism">agnostic</a>, his conversion to Catholicism was, and still is, the subject of much controversy, but his attachment to Saint Thérèse seems to have been sincere: "In some ways, Maurras was a very modern 'Catholic': he was sensitive to the "Little way" of Thérèse of Lisieux.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Alain_Mimoun" title="Alain Mimoun">Alain Mimoun</a> – Algerian-French Olympic marathon champion. He converted from <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> to <a href="/wiki/Catholicism" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholicism">Roman Catholicism</a> in 1955. A few weeks before the <a href="/wiki/1956_Summer_Olympics" title="1956 Summer Olympics">1956 Olympics</a> in <a href="/wiki/Melbourne" title="Melbourne">Melbourne</a>, he had doubts about his ability to win. In desperation, following the advice of one of his friends, he went to Lisieux to the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_St._Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se,_Lisieux" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilica of St. Thérèse, Lisieux">Basilica of St. Thérèse</a>. One month later, he won the <a href="/wiki/1956_Summer_Olympics" title="1956 Summer Olympics">1956 Olympics</a> <a href="/wiki/Marathon" title="Marathon">marathon</a>. From that day on, he was convinced that he owed this victory to the help of Saint Thérèse. Since then, every year on October 1, the feast day of the Saint, he has made the pilgrimage to Lisieux.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> He said: «&#160;St Thérèse of Lisieux is my patron Saint. The white roses which I planted in front of her [her statue in the garden] flower almost all the year round.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGaucher1987220_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGaucher1987220-184">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Moscati" title="Giuseppe Moscati">Giuseppe Moscati</a> – Italian physician and university professor, known for his piety. He was canonised in 1987 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Emmanuel_Mounier" title="Emmanuel Mounier">Emmanuel Mounier</a> - French writer and philosopher wrote about Thérèse of Lisieux: «&#160;At a time when the <a href="/wiki/Petite_bourgeoisie" title="Petite bourgeoisie">petit-bourgeois</a> spirit cannot fail to appear as the dullest antipode of the Christian life, would it not be a ruse of the Holy Spirit, [the coming of Thérèse], a paradox of Mercy to have hidden under these banal appearances the mysteries of the highest flame of love?».<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Papini" title="Giovanni Papini">Giovanni Papini</a> - Italian journalist, novelist, poet and philosopher, initially <a href="/wiki/Anti-clericalism" title="Anti-clericalism">anti-clerical</a>, he converted to Catholicism. He wrote about Thérèse of Lisieux in the posthumous book <i>Il muro dei gelsomini (The Wall of the Jasmine)</i>, published in 1957. One of the stories in the book is entitled <i>The Smile of the Saint</i>. He says that as a child he was marked by the smile of a statue of a young girl, in one of the chapels of the church <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_Maddalena_dei_Pazzi" title="Santa Maria Maddalena dei Pazzi">Santa Maria Maddalena dei Pazzi</a> in <a href="/wiki/Florence" title="Florence">Florence</a>. A few years later, he discovered that this girl was in fact Thérèse of Lisieux, who had come on a pilgrimage to the same church in 1887 with her parents.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Marc_Sangnier" title="Marc Sangnier">Marc Sangnier</a> - Founder of <a href="/wiki/Le_Sillon" title="Le Sillon">Le Sillon</a> - "May Therese from on high support us and show us how to be more one with Jesus."<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Inline_citation#When_you_must_use_inline_citations" title="Wikipedia:Inline citation"><span title="The text near this tag needs a citation. (October 2022)">This quote needs a citation</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Fernando_del_Valle" title="Fernando del Valle">Fernando del Valle</a> - American operatic tenor. He received a Catholic education and attended Brother Martin High School in the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_New_Orleans" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of New Orleans">Roman Catholic Archdiocese of New Orleans</a>. (<span style="color:blue"><i>Concerning his devotion to Thérèse of Lisieux</i> </span>:<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>).</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Maxence_Van_der_Meersch" title="Maxence Van der Meersch">Maxence Van der Meersch</a> -  French Flemish writer of Catholic inspiration. He wrote a religious biography about Thérèse of Lisieux, entitled <i>La petite Sainte Thérèse</i> (1943).</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jean_Vanier" title="Jean Vanier">Jean Vanier</a> - Canadian Catholic philosopher and theologian. In 1964 he founded <a href="/wiki/L%27Arche" title="L&#39;Arche">L'Arche</a>, an international voluntary organisation working primarily for support networks for people with intellectual disabilities. (<span style="color:blue"><i>Concerning his devotion to Thérèse of Lisieux</i> </span>:<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>)</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Works">Works</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_of_Lisieux&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Works">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li>St. Thérèse of Lisieux (1922). <i>The Story of a Soul</i>. Translated by Taylor, Thomas N. (1873-1963) London: Burns, Oates &amp; Washbourne, 1912; 8th ed., 1922)</li></ul>
<p><b>Modern editions and English translations</b>

<li id="cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarrison2003111-70"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHarrison2003111_70-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHarrison2003111_70-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="#CITEREFHarrison2003">Harrison (2003)</a>, p.&#160;111.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-71"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-71">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><i>A Memoir of my Sister</i>, written by Céline Martin, is filled with numerous examples of Therese as teacher:
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>-<i>Céline</i>: Oh! When I think how much I have to acquire!
</p><p>-<i>Therese</i>: Rather, how much you have to lose! Jesus Himself will fill your soul with treasures in the same measure that you move your imperfections out of the way.
And Céline recalled a story Therese told about egotism: "The 28 month old Therese visited <a href="/wiki/Le_Mans" title="Le Mans">Le Mans</a> and was given a basket filled with candies, at the top of which were two sugar rings":
</p><p>- Oh! How wonderful! There is a sugar ring for Céline too!
On her way to the station however the basket overturned, and one of the sugar rings disappeared.
</p><p>- Ah, I no longer have any sugar ring for poor Céline!
</p><p>Reminding me of the incident she observed:
</p><p>
- See how deeply rooted in us is this self-love! Why was it Céline's sugar ring, and not mine, that was lost?.</p></blockquote></span>
</li>

(...) Par son intercession, elle m'a ouvert le jardin de la confiance. Alors j'ai goûté le vrai fruit de l'abandon. De plus, elle m'a fait avancer dans l'esprit d'enfance (...). Et toute son action, inutile de le dire, porte le cachet de l'amour.</i>&#160;», read online: (fr) <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://crc-canada.net/eglise-au-canada/20e-siecle/bse-dina-belanger.html">"La bienheureuse Dina Bélanger sœur Marie Sainte-Cécile de Rome"</a>. 1997<span class="reference-accessdate">.
